Crease: Parryable, E1, 350 (269) / 100 / 480, 6%, edge 5. Mix-up: from a wall face at head height while you watch the drop. Counter: parry on the "tink"; Tempo pins it to the face for 500ms, with no Unfold.
Snap Shut: Breaker, E2, 640 (492) / 150 / 850, 15%, two planes closing on a 3 by 3 cell. Mix-up: a sidestep into a plane is a hit. Counter: the ring shows on both planes; step out of the cell along the pillar, not away from it.
Unfold: mobility, 450 (346) / - / 500, relocates along the nearest pillar face up to 15. Mix-up: it unfolds below you and strikes up through the ledge. Counter: track the paper sound and punish the arrival, its largest open window.
Drop Edge: Breaker, E2, 700 (538) / 150 / 950, 16%, a vertical line 1.5 by 8 from above. Mix-up: it falls where you stood when its startup began. Counter: move at all; it cannot track.
Paper Storm: Unparryable, E2, 760 (585) / 200 / 1000, 10% plus knockback toward the pillar's edge, ring 6. Mix-up: the knockback aims at a Void Pocket edge. Counter: Dash toward the pillar, never away; Blocking it Reels with 6 studs of knockback, so Block only with your back to the pillar. No single hit carries a player over an edge (4.11 Law 3).
Variation: hold on Snap Shut and Drop Edge 0 to 500ms; feints on Crease and Snap Shut 0 to 30% (a feinted Snap Shut is its signature); Breaker to Parryable 1:2 to 2:1; unfold_frequency low, medium or high.
Phases: Crease and Snap Shut on one face; at two-thirds, Unfold across two faces; at one-third, Drop Edge and Paper Storm in the vertical.
Fair tell: it crackles like paper through every startup, directional and louder than any ambient sound, and a Breaker's ring shows through the plane it will strike with.
Elemental: rain, Paper Storm knockback +2 and Soaked; heatwave, Singe on Crease; snow, Chill on Snap Shut.
Lantern-Eater (suited to Alone and Fog Realm ambushes). A thing that eats light: your campfire, your spell glow, your friend's aura. A Grab-heavy ambusher whose kit takes your sight, so its tells are sound and each class cue's afterimage, burned into the fog for the full telegraph. Fog Realm, the Mirrorfog and dark Caverns; slow in the open, fast in fog.
Gulp: Grab, E2, 620 (477) / 150 / 950 whiff, 12%, and for 4s after the hold your own spell effects and aura are invisible to you, capsule 4. Mix-up: it follows a Dim, so the chevrons and the inhale are the only cue. Counter: hit it or Dash; the maw is 4 wide, so aim barely matters.
Dim: Parryable, E1, 380 (292) / 100 / 500, 5% and it puts out every lit source within 6 (campfire, lantern), arc 120 degrees, 5. Mix-up: it Dims your campfire, so a player cooking in the Aether (18.12) fights in the dark. Counter: parry; a parried Eater coughs back the light it ate.
Swallow: Unparryable, E2, 800 (615) / 200 / 1100, 9% plus thick fog around you for 5s (visibility 15 studs), radius 7. Mix-up: the fog hides the next attacker's body, never its class cue, which draws at full brightness. Counter: Dash out; Blocking Reels you 6 studs toward whatever the fog hides.
Blind Lunge: Breaker, E2, 680 (523) / 150 / 900, 14%, line 2 by 10 from outside visibility. Mix-up: only the ring and the "thud-crack" reach you. Counter: the ring shows the line; sidestep it, then hit it in its visible recovery.
Variation: hold on Gulp 0 to 600ms (the longest in the Aether; a waiting Gulp is its signature); feints on Dim 0 to 25%; Grab to Parryable 1:2 to 1:1; ambush_distance 12 to 25 studs.
Phases: Dim and Blind Lunge; at two-thirds, Gulp; at one-third, Swallow, alternating with Gulp.
Fair tell: every class cue is the one light it does not eat, and its wet breathing grows louder as it closes. It may hide its body, never its intent.
Elemental: void mist, Gulp drains 10% mana; rain, Blind Lunge +3 and Soaked; heatwave, its hits Singe, and a Singed player can see it for the Singe's duration.
The Unformed (suited to group play on open terrain). A blind giant that never finished forming, feeling for you with mostly Unparryable limbs. Slow and enormous, it asks groups a spacing question, with the floor's edge as the real threat on Void Pockets, and it carries the Aether's heaviest Calamity slam. Infinite Plane, Void Pockets, Mountainous flats and the Convergence's largest arena.
Feel: Parryable, E2, 620 (477) / 200 sweeping / 800, 11%, arc 180 degrees, 10, 1.5 high. Mix-up: it sweeps toward the player who just dashed (it tracks moved air). Counter: parry it (Tempo makes it recoil), or Leap over it.
Palm: Unparryable, E2, 780 (600) / 150 / 1100, 17%, circle 6. Mix-up: it lands on a player standing still to cast an Echelon 2 spell, and it is just slower than most Echelon 2 startups. Counter: cast from 7 studs or cast E1 spells; Dash out.
Haul: Grab, E2, 640 (492) / 200 / 1000 whiff, 14% then a throw of 8 (toward an edge within 12, never across it), capsule 5. Mix-up: it grabs a player in Parry Recovery after a whiffed Feel parry. Counter: hit it in startup, a job for allies; a Haul costs damage and position, never a death.
Collapse: Unparryable, E3, 1400 (1077) / 250 / 1600, 26%, ring 14 with 4 studs of outward knockback. Mix-up: it Groans toward the loudest caster first, so the group's heaviest mage is the center. Counter: everyone leaves 14 studs, and the recovery is the burst window; never stand between it and an edge when the sky darkens.
Groan: reposition, 900 / - / 600, it turns to the loudest player (most mana spent in the last 3s). Mix-up: readable and exploitable, so a cheap caster can pull it off a friend. Counter: none needed; it is the group's tool. Drawing its aggro is control under 5.5: a stronger non-peer who pulls it off you voids your catalyst.
Variation: hold on Palm 0 to 600ms; Unparryable to Parryable 2:1 to 3:1; it never feints (it is blind and does not bluff). At 0.8x it is the Aether's slowest fight and at 1.3x still its most readable. Scaling raises hold and Palm share only.
Phases: Feel and Palm; at two-thirds, Haul and Groan tracking; at one-third, Collapse, at most once per 25s.
Fair tell: its head turns toward the sound it will strike, and its body creaks like ice under weight through the whole startup.
Elemental: snow, Chill on Feel (stricter Leap timing); rain, the Palm leaves a puddle for 3s that shocks for 3% if the Unformed is lightning-buffed; void mist, Collapse drains 10% mana inside the ring in place of 4% of its damage.
The Weft (suited to groups in Caverns and Overgrown). A loom-spider of grey thread whose web is the arena. Threads disable Step and Dash where they lie and tether players to each other, so a group must cut each other free; it teaches that mobility is a resource the Aether can take. Caverns, Overgrown and the Endless Green. The web is pre-laid, grey and humming.
Needle: Parryable, E1, 350 (269) / 100 / 500, 6%, thrust 6. Mix-up: from above the thread you are about to cut. Counter: parry; a parried Weft drops one thread.
Cast Thread: Unparryable, E1, 500 (385) / projectile / 700, 3% plus a thread line 1 wide for 6s (Step and Dash disabled on it; any Strike or E1 cuts it), a projectile line of 12. Mix-up: laid across your dash lane during a Breaker startup. Counter: cut the line, and keep lanes clear as a group chore.
Tether: Grab, E2, 600 (462) / 150 / 900 whiff, 10% then a 5s tether to the nearest ally within 10 (moving apart drains both players' mana or Strain, 2% per second), capsule 3. Mix-up: it tethers the healer to the melee, then Shuttles between them. Counter: hit it in startup or Dash; a tethered pair that moves together takes nothing.
Shuttle: Breaker, E2, 660 (508) / 200 moving / 900, 15%, along one thread, 2 wide. Mix-up: it runs the thread you stand beside. Counter: that thread carries the ring through the whole startup; step off it, never along it.
Draw Tight: Unparryable, E2, 800 (615) / 150 / 1100, 12% plus a 4-stud pull toward the Weft for every tethered or thread-standing player, web-wide. Mix-up: it pulls thread-standers into Needle range. Counter: get off threads; tethered pairs step together, then off.
Variation: thread_density 4 to 10 threads per cast; hold on Shuttle 0 to 500ms; feints on Needle 0 to 30%; Parryable to Unparryable 2:1 to 1:1.
Phases: Needle and Cast Thread build the web; at two-thirds, Shuttle and Tether; at one-third, Draw Tight, re-laying threads after each.
Fair tell: every thread it will use hums first, in the pitch of its class sound; the web is an instrument the player learns to hear.
Elemental: rain, a lightning Weft's threads shock 2% on contact; heatwave, any fire E1 burns threads, and its hits Singe in return; void mist, Tether drains mana instead of Strain for mages.
Vowed note: a Fist on threads cannot Step but can still Strike, and Strikes cut threads, so the Weft is a spacing exam, not a wall. Stepping off every thread makes a vowed player Winded by about the fifth (3.4); cutting is the answer.
The Wader (suited to Alone or pairs, Acid Pools and Quarters). A tall, hooded figure that walks on acid as on stone, using its staff as a pole. Every hit wants you a step closer to the acid: the Aether's hazards are part of every Horror's kit. Acid Pools, the Weeping Dark and acid crossings on Quarters borders. It patrols the safe route and makes it unsafe.
Pole Thrust: Parryable, E1, 360 (277) / 100 / 500, 6% plus a 1-stud push, thrust 7. Mix-up: three blocked thrusts walk a player to the edge. Counter: parry (a parry has no knockback, 4.6) or step around; never Block repeatedly near acid.
Edge Sweep: Breaker, E2, 660 (508) / 200 / 900, 13% plus a 3-stud push away from it, arc 180 degrees, 6. Mix-up: the push feels safe and is toward the pool behind you. Counter: sidestep, and fight with the acid behind it, which is the whole fight.
Carry: Grab, E2, 620 (477) / 150 / 950 whiff, 11%, then it walks 3 studs with you (toward acid within 6, never into it) and drops you, capsule 3. Mix-up: position, not damage, is the cost. Counter: hit it in startup or Dash; after a Carry, use its recovery.
Splash: Unparryable, E2, 760 (585) / 200 / 1000, 10% plus an acid splash of 4 (contact 3% per 0.5s for 2s), radius 5. Mix-up: it throws acid onto the safe route. Counter: Dash out; the splash has the green sheen and hiss of all Aether acid (10.13).
Vigil: stance, E0, 300 / 1200 / 300, it plants the staff and reflects the next Echelon 1 projectile, never Echelon 2. Mix-up: an E1 fired into it comes back. Counter: cast an E2, or walk around and strike; it never Vigils twice in a row.
Variation: hold on Edge Sweep and Carry 0 to 500ms; feints on Pole Thrust 0 to 30%; Breaker to Parryable 1:2 to 1:1; vigil_frequency low, medium or high.
Phases: Pole Thrust and Edge Sweep; then Carry and Vigil; then Splash, thrown on the route behind you to fight you forward.
Fair tell: the acid ripples toward the point it will strike through the whole startup, and its hood turns to the acid before a Carry.
Elemental: rain, Splash reaches 5 and Vigil reflects water E1s with the +10% relation; heatwave, acid steam cuts visibility near pools by 30%; void mist, Carry drains mana.
Fair Danger: no Wader action places a player in acid directly; every acid death traces to where the player stood when the push came.
The Likeness (suited to Alone; the Hostile Adaptation Horror). A copy of you that got the details wrong. It casts in your strongest element at your echelon (chosen by Hostile Adaptation, 10.7) and carries a true void Calamity, so it Contests you where every point of mana you feed is gone for good. Against a vowed player it fights with a crude copy of their discipline (Strikes and one Technique) plus the Calamity. Any terrain; it arrives when the player stays in one place (10.9), and bows. It pressures and never neutralizes: a fire specialist meets a water Likeness (-10% in clashes), never an immunity.
Mirror Bolt: Parryable, E1, 340 (262), spell floor 250 / the spell's own / 480, 6, the Echelon 1 spell of your element (e1_firebolt, e1_shard, e1_arc, e1_windslash or e1_tidebolt; an earth Likeness uses e1_stonegrip as its Grab instead). Mix-up: it mirrors your casting cadence from the last 10s. Counter: change your rhythm, or clash it for the Last Word, which it never takes because it only mirrors.
Mirror Lance: Parryable projectile or Breaker beam by spell, E2, 620 (477), spell floor 450 / the spell's own / 850, 14, e2_fireball, e2_icelance or e2_torrent by your element. Mix-up: fired as you release yours, forcing a Contest it will feed. Counter: Dash to break off when the lock drifts toward you, or release early and dodge the residual; a Contest won here is a Durability catalyst with real stakes.
Wrong Hand: Grab, E2, 600 (462) / 150 / 900 whiff, 12% plus a mana drain equal to your last spell's cost (at most 8% of max), capsule 3. Mix-up: opened in the recovery of your big spell, when you cannot cancel. Counter: a Barrier parry, any hit or a Dash; cast big spells from 7 studs.
Unmaking (casts e3_unmaking): Parryable projectile and Unparryable area, E3, 1250 (962) / 250 / 1500, 24%, projectile radius 2 at the Echelon 3 tier, detonation radius 8. Mix-up: the sky tell comes while a Mirror Lance is in flight. Counter: a Calamity of your own destroys the Lance and continues (4.8); otherwise Dash the Lance and dodge the area, or parry the projectile at a mana cost (4.6). Surviving it grants full Insight; it is the Aether's own Calamity source.
Mirror Barrier (e0_barrier): defensive, held up to 1000ms, 150 release lag, shell radius 3.5, block drain by 4.5. Mix-up: raised the moment you commit a Technique or an E2, so you drain it and stand in recovery. Counter: alternate Breakers with Grabs (4.11 Law 5); a Fist's Grab ignores it.
Variation: mirror_lag 300 to 900ms (how closely it copies your cadence); feints on Mirror Bolt 0 to 30%, paying the feint tax visibly (it flinches when it cancels); hold on Mirror Lance 0 to 500ms. Its mix is your own play, clamped so at least 25% of its actions are Wrong Hand and Unmaking, and so a vowed player's Likeness keeps Strike to Technique to Grab at 3:1:1.
Phases: Mirror Bolt and Mirror Barrier while it learns your cadence (long mirror_lag); at two-thirds, Mirror Lance and Wrong Hand with a shorter lag; at one-third, Unmaking once per 30s, during which it stops mirroring.
Fair tell: its shape is yours but its light is void-black where your element glows. Its spells are yours, so you already know every telegraph, and its own actions use the standard cues.
Elemental: weather adds only its status (snow, Chill on Mirror Bolt; rain, a water Likeness's clashes +10%; heatwave, Singe; void mist, Wrong Hand's cap rises to 12%).
Drops: a Tome fragment of the void Calamity (the Aether's own fragment source), under the shared drop rule. Mages chase its "Bane of" Epithet.

11. The Overworld
11.1 The Gradient (PROPOSED)
The overworld is the Aether's gentler edge, not a separate set of rules. A gradient from learnable to unlearnable gives players a risk dial:
Anchors: towns and training grounds. Stable and reliable.
The Drift: regions whose states shift over time (11.4).
Bleed Zones: places where Aether rules leak in, near rifts and ruins (11.5).
The Aether: fully unlearnable.
11.2 Anchors (PROPOSED)
New players still need predictable training spots, especially at low population. Anchors host cities and towns, Inns, the Seer, mentors, and reliable PvE training. They have no hotspot grinding (see spawn budgets in 11.4).
11.3 Mana Zones (LOCKED)
High Density (Leylines): fast regen, larger spells. PvP hotspots.
Dead Zones: zero regen. Wellstone artifacts required to survive.
Wellstones (LOCKED, v3.3): an equipped artifact that stores mana, Small (150) or Large (400) (tune), and heavy (17.8). It discharges only where ambient regen is zero (Dead Zones, and a whole region during Deadcalm, 11.13). There it stands in for the missing ambient mana: it restores your normal regeneration (rapid out of combat, the normal trickle in combat) and pays for it from its charge. It never exceeds normal regen, so it is never better than standing outside the Dead Zone, and it is not a mana potion (24.4). Each point restored costs more than a point of charge: Raw Dead-Zone Efficiency wastes 50%, Perfected wastes 10% (tune, Law 2 curve). Getting more out of the same charge is what trains Dead-Zone Efficiency (5.8). Charging: sit at a leyline out of combat (a Small fills in about 2 real minutes, tune), or pour in your own mana out of combat at a 30% loss (tune), which trains nothing (3.1). Inert everywhere else, including the whole Aether. Charge reads by glow and hum pitch, never a number. Enchanters make them from Leystone and crystals (17.11). Vowed characters never need one.
Mana Pressure: high-Echelon zones crush low-stat players, draining mana and health rapidly (natural gating).
These double as geography: Leylines suit mages, Dead Zones suit sword-first and vowed cultures (7.3), and Pressure Peaks hold ancient beasts.
Pressure rating (PROPOSED, v3): each Pressure zone carries an echelon rating (4.7). Characters below that echelon's capacity threshold are crushed. Vowed physical characters are checked against the Body threshold instead (5.19.6) and take pressure at 50% (tune) when below it (v3.1; checked against Capacity, a vowed master was crushed everywhere).
Placement (PROPOSED): Leylines and most Dead Zones are part of a region's fixed bones and never drift, because guild leyline control (5.16) needs stable ground. Their strength may vary with the Drift state (11.12).
11.4 The Drift (LOCKED concept, details PROPOSED)
The problem with Deepwoken is fixed hotspots that players farm. The Drift removes reliable coordinates: regions change over time, so "where do I grind" becomes "how do I explore." A grassland may be ocean on the next visit, and a week later a grassland over an ancient civilization with void winds and dangerous mobs. Days later it is gone again.
Duration (confirmed): all Drift durations are real time (3.6). The shortest state lasts 30 minutes. Others last 12 hours, a day, a couple of days, with a maximum of 2 weeks. Recommended weighting: about half last 30 minutes to a few hours, about a third last up to a day, a small share last multiple days, and 1 to 2 week states are rare enough to remember.
Implementation (PROPOSED)
Seeded states: each Drift cell's state (11.12) comes from a seed (cell ID plus time window), so everyone on a shard sees the same thing, and any state can be reproduced for debugging.
Variable lengths: split time into fixed 2-week blocks, and have the seed generate a sequence of states and durations that fills each block. Any server computes the current state from the clock alone, with no saving or syncing.
Occupancy extension is local (v3.1): an occupied cell keeps its state up to the cap (11.12) while the seeded schedule runs on. When the cell clears or the cap ends, it decays and moves straight to the state the clock now names. Only the extension start time is kept, in server memory.
Premade states: each Drift cell has 2 to 4 hand-built states (for example grassland, ocean, grassland over ruins), swapped by seed. Reuse the same ruin kits across regions.
World shards (PROPOSED, v3.1; resolves the per-server versus shared schedule question): Roblox servers start and close all day, so per-server world state would vanish with each server and hopping would reroll the Drift. The overworld instead runs a fixed set of named shards (tune: 8). Each new server joins a shard, reads that shard's schedule seed and event outcomes from MemoryStore (mirrored to DataStore at block end), and writes outcomes back, so shard state outlives any server. Shards differ from each other, so no single Discord tracker solves the world. Spawn budgets and per-account diminishing returns still blunt hopping. Wherever this document says "on a server" about world state (occupied towns, first clears, first-to-master Epithets), read "on a shard". Shard names (plain sea words) and how players hear them: 27.2 (v3.3).
Readable endings: states do not change while someone is inside, up to the occupancy cap (11.12). A short decay phase comes first (spawns stop, then signs appear: flickering mana, fading color, animals leaving). The change completes out of sight, and something is left behind (a mana stain) so players can tell something was here.
Spawn budgets: each state holds a fixed budget of mobs and rewards. A gold mine holds a set amount before it is mined out, even if the state continues. Short states can have higher reward density, and long states lean toward exploration, lore and secrets. Spawns come from weighted tables, not fixed spawners.
Tells: subtle signs before a shift or reveal (a Mana Vision shimmer, animals leaving, a color drift, a low hum). Skilled explorers learn to read them.
Safety: dangerous reveals are layered. A sign comes first, then the threat, so a first death feels like the player's mistake.
11.5 Bleed Zones (PROPOSED)
Places where Aether rules leak in, typically near rifts and ruins:
Exploration pressure: staying in one place draws mobs you have never fought (the Aether rule, intensity turned down).
Light Hostile Adaptation: a small share of spawns lean against your most-used element. Capped, never a hard counter.
Deceptive flora: plants look edible and are not. In the overworld, danger is learnable through Foraging, mentors and Seer lore. In the Aether, nothing is learnable.
Variety supply: they provide the varied sources Learning by Suffering needs.
11.6 Fissures (PROPOSED; failure cost LOCKED, v3.3, was OPEN)
Small, seeded Aether pockets that open in Bleed Zones: a short run (tune: about 10 minutes) with one lighter Horror and Aether-only rewards such as Tome fragments. Fissures teach the Aether's telegraph language before a first real visit, which helps the cold-read fairness problem. They are clearly signposted before entry. Failure (LOCKED, v3.3, was recommended in v3.1): a failed run ejects the player Hollow with Trauma (3.2) and loses its rewards; only a death after the ejection tell costs a Life. Fissures are practice, and players at 1 Life would skip practice that costs a Life.
The Thinling (LOCKED, v3.3; Apprentice band, the Fissure Horror). The first Horror most players meet: a thin, grey, faceless biped of a person's height. It teaches the four parry classes and the Aether's telegraph grammar honestly: one attack per class, each wearing the canonical cue (4.9, 4.13), at baseline timing on every attack. No phases, no feints and the narrowest variation in the game, so a player who fails here failed a reading test, not a dice roll. Its pocket is one module with two exits and no acid or void, with 10.13's grammar in miniature (a mist lip at the edge, a Rift hum at the exit). Kit lines follow 14.1; startup is base (at 1.1x). Values tune.
Lesson One (a straight slap): Parryable, E1, 400 (364) / 100 / 600, 5%, arc 90 degrees, 4. Mix-up: its only one, a hold at Telegraph end of a fixed 300ms. Counter: parry on the drop, not the raise; it acts out Tempo broadly so the reward for a read is obvious.
Lesson Two (a shoulder charge): Breaker, E2, 750 (682) / 150 / 1000, 12%, line 2 by 6. Mix-up: it charges after you Block the slap, so a player's first Breaker Reel happens here, with chain protection and a long recovery to think in. Counter: the cracked ring, the "thud-crack" and the dropped shoulder: sidestep.
Lesson Three (a stomp): Unparryable, E2, 850 (773) / 150 / 1200, 10%, radius 5. Mix-up: it stomps when you stand still after a parry to admire it. Counter: leave 5 studs; every Unparryable in the Aether draws this decal.
Lesson Four (a reach): Grab, E1, 600 (545) / 150 / 800 whiff, 6%, capsule 3. Mix-up: it follows a successful parry, teaching that Grabs answer parry habits before the Mourner (10.14) teaches it for keeps. Counter: a hit in startup, a Dash or a Barrier parry; a Block fails, at 6% rather than 13%.
Variation: speed 0.9x to 1.1x only; no delay variance beyond the fixed hold; no feints; one attack of each class in a rotating order re-rolled per Fissure (a wiki can list the four, not the order). Hidden scaling is off. It pauses 800ms between attacks, long enough to try an answer. Nothing in the Aether is faster, and nothing there pauses longer. It shows each class by its cue and nothing else, so players learn to trust the cue rather than the body.
Fissures roll no Elementals. Thinlings that an Aether Tide (11.13) walks into the overworld keep this kit and timing as the Tide's reading practice.
Drops: one Tome fragment per run, always (a family hinted by the Bleed Zone's lean), and Mana Crystals 1 to 2.
Ejection: the Thinling's killing blow ejects instead of killing. Its tell is the pocket's mist lip rushing inward over about 2s (tune) while the Thinling stops attacking.
11.7 Weather Fronts and Seasons (PROPOSED)
Forecastable weather: overworld weather mirrors the Aether's (rain, mana storm, fog) but can be predicted by the Seer, inn gossip or a trained eye, so players plan around it.
Elemental fronts: snow, heatwave and void mist move through regions on rough paths, bringing mobs of that element. Players can chase one for rewards or avoid it.
Long seasons: 1 to 2 week Drift states can be region-wide events, such as a flood that splits the lowlands into islands.
Fronts can escalate into Great Front events, and long seasons into Great events (11.13).
11.8 Exploration Design (PROPOSED)
The expedition loop: mana is stamina, hunger and thirst drain, and Soul Damage needs a secure bed. That is an extraction loop: pack, push into the unknown, decide when to turn back, with the Inn as home base. Routes are mana puzzles (flight costs mana, pressure zones drain it, dead zones stop regen).
Builds see different maps: a mage can fly over a chasm a sword mage walks around.
Landmarks and tells: visible far-off silhouettes (a mana light pillar, a floating ruin) pull players. Secrets and shifting regions get subtle tells. No map markers.
Rewards that are not XP: Spell Tomes, glyph sources, hidden mentors, techniques (such as Split Focus), shortcuts and Inn locations. This keeps exploration from becoming a farm route.
Rumors instead of quest markers: Inns and the Seer give cryptic, time-limited hints ("lanterns over the marsh this week"). They expire with the state, so wikis cannot hold them.
Grimoire memory: inherited map marks for places that may no longer exist (Section 9.3).
Secure beds in the wild (OPEN): craftable or consumable camps could extend expeditions at PvP risk. See 18.12 for the tended-hearth proposal.
11.9 Dungeons (LOCKED)
Dungeons are seamless and not instanced. A team can seal a Boss Room door. Outside players can break the seal, but only with a massive, coordinated Outpouring (a mana-density check, see Section 13). The sealing team can always unseal from inside, so a sealed room is never a trap for the team that sealed it (LOCKED, v3.3, 4.9).
11.10 Flight (LOCKED)
0 to 100 studs above ground: normal drain.
100 to 200 studs: exponential drain.
200 studs and above: instant exhaustion. (v3.1: restated from feet, at about 1 stud per foot; tune.)
Measuring "above ground" (LOCKED, v3.3): height is measured above a ground reference, not a raw downward raycast. The reference is the highest surface within 80 studs (tune), read from a coarse height grid (16-stud cells) baked once per region (bones never drift, 11.12), plus small grids for state kits. Over the sea it is sea level. The reference falls at most 20 studs per second (tune), so flying off a cliff shrinks the margin instead of exhausting you at once. In the Aether the reference is the layer below; over a void with no ground in range it decays at the same rate, down to the layout's lowest island level. Altitude Tolerance (5.10) raises both lines by up to +60% at Perfected (tune), keeping the band width, under a hard world ceiling for everyone (tune: Y = 1,200). The rising drain reads as frost on the mana bar's edge and thinning, whistling wind.
LOD: players fade at extreme distances and appear as glowing mana orbs.
11.11 Regional Template and Scope (PROPOSED)
Build 3 to 4 regions from one template: one mana-zone type, one faction or culture, one bestiary threat, one survival challenge, and one secret that connects to the Aether. Ten to fifteen handmade set pieces beat large procedural filler. Factions can be schools of thought (efficiency and suppression, burst and overcharge, a hermit order of suffering, a sword lineage that treats mana as a crutch) who disagree about what the Aether is.
The launch world realizes this template (v3.3): the Holdfast's four regions are in 27.2 and 27.3, the schools in 27.6, and the handmade set pieces in 27.5.
11.12 Overworld Construction and Terrain (PROPOSED, v3)
The overworld is handmade, then varied by the Drift. It is not procedurally generated. Its variation is learnable (Design Law 3); the Aether's is not (10.13).
Shape of the world
The launch world is an archipelago: 3 to 4 large island regions (11.11) on one streamed map, separated by sea channels crossed by boat, swimming or flight. The eviction rule (17.4) washes players ashore on small outer islands. At launch this is the Holdfast: four regions and the Outer Shoals (27.2, 27.3), with example Drift cells in 27.4 (v3.3).
Region size (tune): about 2,000 by 2,000 studs. Each region is divided into Drift cells (tune: about 400 by 400 studs). A cell is the unit that changes state.
Anchor cells (towns, Inns, the Seer, training grounds) never drift.
Bones never drift; skin does
Each cell has four layers:
Layer 1. Bones: the fixed silhouette. Coastlines, mountains, major rivers, landmarks, leylines and dungeon entrances. Built once in Roblox Terrain and fixed parts. Never changes, so navigation and landmarks stay learnable.
Layer 2. State: 2 to 4 hand-built state kits per cell (grassland, flooded, ruins revealed, overgrown, frozen). Built as part and mesh models, not voxel edits, and swapped by seed (11.4).
Layer 3. Dressing: seeded scatter of flora, rocks, forage nodes and ingredients (Section 18), placed by biome rule tables (density, slope limits, clearance from paths), deterministic per state instance.
Layer 4. Life: mobs and rewards from weighted spawn tables with per-state budgets (11.4).
Roblox notes: runtime voxel writes (Terrain:WriteVoxels) are expensive to compute and replicate, so states never rewrite voxels. Flooded states use a raised water part with swim volumes over fixed terrain, not new Terrain water. Far landmarks use low-detail imposters (model streaming LOD) so the silhouette is visible from anywhere in the region.
Drift cell rules
Through-routes: every cell edge has fixed entry and exit points, and every state must keep them walkable. A state change never traps, strands or walls anyone in.
Safe edges: no state may put a lethal drop or hazard within a set distance of an Anchor or a through-route (tune: 40 studs).
Occupancy: a cell does not change while a player is inside or within a buffer (tune: 100 studs) (11.4). To stop a player holding a rich state forever by standing in it, occupancy can extend a state only up to a cap (tune: 30 minutes). After the cap the cell enters its decay phase: spawns and rewards stop and exploration pressure rises until it is empty.
Reveals: some states open a dungeon entrance, a Bleed Zone, a Fissure site or a hidden mentor. These are authored into the state kit, not random.
Seeds: state = hash(schedule seed, cell ID, 2-week block index), as in 11.4. The schedule seed is per shard (11.4). A debug command previews any cell at any time on any seed.
Mana zones in the bones: leylines and most Dead Zones are fixed (11.3). Their strength and the pressure rating of Pressure Peaks may vary by state.
Bleed Zones and Fissures: a Bleed Zone is a cell state that carries Aether rules (11.5). Fissure openings are seeded points inside those states (11.6).
Budgets
Each cell has a budget for parts, lights, emitters, and concurrent mobs (tune against low-end mobile). Mobile clients receive dressing at reduced density (tune: 50%) with no gameplay-relevant objects removed: forage nodes, cover and hazards are always identical across platforms.
Validation (run on every state kit before it ships)
Pathfinding between all through-routes and from every Inn to every cell exit.
No spawn points inside geometry or within sight of an Inn door.
Budget counts per state on low-end settings.
No walkable gaps into kill planes.
Flight and climb parity: any area that requires flight to reach holds nothing a vowed character needs, and vowed-only routes (climbs, crawlspaces) hold nothing a mage needs. Different builds see different maps, never better ones.
11.13 World Events (PROPOSED, v3)
The Drift changes places slowly. World Events change what is happening right now. They give players a reason to be somewhere together, rotate which archetype gets the spotlight, and leave marks on the world. They are never fixed timers to farm.
Three scales
Local events (minutes): one Drift cell, 10 to 20 real minutes (tune), several per region per hour. Sized for a 15-minute mobile session (2.2).
Regional events (about an hour): one region, 30 to 90 real minutes (tune), a few per server per day. Night-bound Regional events run from dusk to dawn instead (about 30 minutes, 3.6).
Seasonal events (hours to days): one region, lasting a Drift state. They use the shared world schedule, so every player meets them.
Great events (days): authored arcs that run on every server at once, rare (tune: one per 2-week Drift block). Contribution is tallied across servers, and the outcome changes the world for the next block.
Framework rules
Seeded schedule: Local and Regional events come from the same kind of seed as the Drift (11.4), hash(schedule seed, region, time window), drawing from weighted tables. Great events are planned by the team as live operations and revealed only through tells.
Sign, then threat (11.4): every event announces itself before it starts. Local: signs a short while before (tune: 30 to 60s), such as birds scattering, a smoke column, a mana flicker. Regional: a rumor at Inns and from the Seer ahead of time (tune: 30 to 60 real minutes), then a sky or landscape sign shortly before. Great: days of escalating signs.
No map markers (11.8). Landmarks point the way: a light pillar, a smoke column, a sky that changes color.
Learnable grammar: event types, their tells and their rules are fixed and learnable (Design Law 3). Exact times and places are not.
Rewards: knowledge, tools, materials, ingredients, Tome fragments, glyph sources, cosmetics, ledger entries and Epithets (5.22). Never raw stats. XP flows through the normal model (5.2): event-only mobs give Novelty XP, and Familiarity and Exposure still apply. Each event holds a fixed reward and XP budget that does not grow with the crowd (5.6).
Rumor reach: Local and Regional schedules are per shard (11.4), so a rumor heard at an Inn holds on every server of that shard. Seasonal and Great events are shared and reach everyone.
Anti-hopping: per-account diminishing returns for the same event type across servers (tune: the second 50%, the third 10%, reset after 12 real hours).
Archetype rotation: each Regional event carries a lean (mage, physical, explorer, social, hearth). The weekly schedule balances leans, so no archetype has the world's attention every day.
Fair Danger: deaths in events are ordinary overworld deaths (a Life, 9.1). Protected buildings stay protected (13). Events that bring Aether rules into the overworld say so with Bleed Zone tells (11.5).
Performance: event mob caps and VFX budgets per server (20.2). Great events arrive in staggered waves.
Event catalog (launch candidates, tune)
Aether Tide (Regional; mage and physical; team): at night Bleed Zones swell, Fissures open in clusters, and lighter Horrors walk the overworld under the Horror Combat Standard (4.9). Players hold the line at towns. Rewards Tome fragments, and practice reading Horrors before a first real Aether visit.
Leyline Surge (Regional; mage): a leyline flares. Mana regeneration and spell size swell along it, Echelon 3 casts cost less inside it (tune: 20%), and guilds contest it (5.16). Mana Beasts swarm toward it, which gives cooks Resonant meat (18.7).
Deadcalm (Regional; physical): the region goes dead. Every zone counts as a Dead Zone for the event (tune: 45 minutes). Mages ration Wellstones while vowed characters own the field (7.3). Dead Zone beasts surface.
Migration (Regional; explorer and hearth): a herd of Mana Beasts crosses the region on a route that differs every time. It supplies rare cuts, tramples camps in its path, and draws predators behind it.
Great Front (Regional; survival): an escalated weather front (blizzard, firestorm, void squall) sweeps a path (11.7). Passes close, Warmth and Cooling meals matter (18.10), and so do Climate leanings (3.5).
Drought and Blight (Seasonal; survival; lasting a Drift state, hours to days): a drought dries wells and ponds, so water is scarce and shallow-well characters feel it first. A blight spoils crops, so food is scarce and big-stomach characters feel it first (3.5). Inn staples cost more. Larders and preserved rations shine (18.8).
Falling Star (Local to Regional; race): a meteor strikes. The crater holds starmetal, the material for spirit-forged blades (7.3), and sometimes a Horror crawls out of it. The crater stays for the rest of the Drift block.
Risen Ruin (Regional; explorer): a rare Drift reveal raises an ancient or floating ruin with a temporary dungeon (11.9). The first full clear on a server becomes a ledger Epithet.
Bounty Season (Regional; law): a town posts bounties on its wanted list for one in-game day, dusk to dusk (about an hour). Heat decays slower, Marshals patrol, and bounty hunters are paid from criminals' assets (12).
Wandering Master (Regional; rare): a hidden mentor, often a Fist master (5.19.8), appears somewhere a rumor hints at, for 60 to 90 real minutes (tune). Only those who find them can learn from them.
Hearth Fair (Regional; social, the rest-day event): a town holds a cooking festival with judged rounds (NPC judges with their own cravings, 3.5), a Feast (18.13) and small games. Rewards are cosmetics, recipe cards and a ledger line.
Siege (Regional or Great; team): a faction army or a Horror host marches on a town. Players defend walls and wards (13). If the town falls, it is Occupied for the rest of the Drift block. Its Inn still works (protected), but its market, mentors and guards are replaced by the occupiers, and retaking it becomes the next event.
Great events (examples)
The Long Night: an Aether Tide on every region, every in-game night for three real days. The global count of sealed Fissures decides whether the Tide recedes, or a Bleed Zone becomes permanent for the next block.
The Parting: a Great Front that splits a region into islands for two weeks (11.7 long seasons).
Results are never shown as a scoreboard. They show through the world: which towns are lit, what the Seer and Inn rumors say, what changed on the next block.
Persistent marks
Events leave marks for the rest of the Drift block: a crater, a burned forest, an occupied town, a new rumor. Marks expire with the block, so the world remembers for a while and then heals (13).
Technical notes
An event director on each server reads the seed and runs events with server authority. Great-event tallies use MemoryStore with eventual consistency, and each server reads them back on a slow cadence (tune: every minute).
Events never start on a server that is scheduled to shut down. Event participation that feeds the ledger is saved to the character profile with the next normal save.
Build order: after the Drift (21.2). Ship Local events first, then two or three Regional types, then Great events.
11.14 The Sea: Swimming, Ferries and Channels (LOCKED, v3.3)
The archipelago (11.12) is crossed by swimming, ferries, boats or flight. Vowed characters cannot fly (7.3), so the sea must always have a way across on foot and by fin.
Swimming: sea channels are Roblox Terrain water (bones), so native swimming works there. Flooded Drift states use water parts with swim volumes (11.12) and a small custom swim controller tuned to feel the same. Speed is about 60% of walking (tune). Sprint-swimming builds Strain (3.4) for everyone. While swimming a character cannot Block, Parry, Dash or Step, or cast Echelon 1 or higher; cantrips are allowed, and a mage may take flight from the water at +50% cost (tune). Heavy armor or the Overladen band (17.8) sinks you, and deep water drowns. Removing armor in water takes 2s (tune): it goes to the pack if there is room, otherwise it floats in a crate for 5 minutes. There is no air bar: after about 15s underwater (tune), Blood drains 1% per second (tune), with muffled audio, bubbles and a closing vignette as the cues. Seawater satisfies nothing and drains Blood if drunk; boiling does not make it safe (18.4 covers pond water only). This is learnable (Design Law 3).
Ferries: each region's Anchor harbor joins a ferry loop through every region and outer island (tune: a departure every 5 real minutes, 60 to 90s crossings). The fare is 1 Mana Crystal (tune), and leaving an outer island is free (eviction mercy, 17.4). Passengers are seated and the ferry moves on a server-driven path, which avoids carrying loose characters on moving platforms. Leaving the seat means jumping into the sea. Ferries are protected (13), and crimes aboard count as a settled area (12).
Channel classes (tune): Shallows (up to 60 studs: wade or swim, no danger); Narrows (up to 250 studs, about 60s of swimming, no sea beasts); Open channels (over 250 studs: ferry, boat or flight, with sea beasts that give a sign first, a fin or a wake, 11.4). Every outer island has a Narrows to a region or a ferry stop. Every pair of regions is on the ferry loop. No region or Anchor is reachable only by flight (11.12 parity). Flying an Open channel is possible but long, which gives flight Duration (5.10) its purpose. In the Aether, Quarters water borders already guarantee a crossing (10.13), and there are no boats.
Rowboats (post-launch): bought at a harbor, one per slot, summoned at any dock, 2 to 4 seats, slow. Rowing builds Strain. A rowboat is a tier-1 wooden structure (13). Sailboats come later still.

12. Law, Crime and Bounties (PROPOSED, concept confirmed)
Towns and cities have their own police forces and armies. Villains are hunted.
Investigation: crimes leave Residual Mana Signatures that guards can read. Mana Suppression hides yours, giving villains a use for it.
Jurisdiction: heat is per town or region, decays over play time (3.6), and needs a witness or a signature trace. Crimes in the wilds do not count.
Escalation ladder: warning, guards, hunter squads tuned to counter your archetype, a named Marshal as a duel boss, then waves of reinforcements (12.2 to 12.5, v3.3).
Escape routes: leave the jurisdiction, break sight and suppress your aura, pay a fine, or hide in Dead Zones and Bleed Zones where mage guards are weak.
Surrender: submitting means fines, confiscation or jail instead of death (12.5, v3.3).
Guard kills cost a Life (OPEN, recommended yes): otherwise villains have no stakes. Warnings must be clear, since a crime is a choice and the player always has notice and a way out.
PvP definition (OPEN, recommended): crimes against players in settled areas raise heat. The wilds do not. This gives victims recourse, and player bounty hunters become a role.
Anti-abuse: guards give almost no XP, so villain loops are not farms. Bounties are paid from the criminal's assets, so alts cannot launder them. Guards target only the wanted player, so nobody can kite them onto others.
Performance: small squads arriving in waves, limited pathfinding calls, and a cap on concurrent guards per server.
12.1 Guards: Shared Rules (LOCKED, v3.3)
Guards drop nothing edible (18.7) and no crystals, give almost no XP, target only the wanted player, and arrive in waves under the concurrent-guard cap (20.2). Mage guards never fight in Dead Zones or Bleed Zones, where the escape routes lie; trackers and physical squads follow there. Every guard action carries its class marker and sits above its startup and recovery floors. Kit lines follow 14.1, and every value is (tune). Guards are human-scale, cast table spells (4.13) and use Body techniques, so Learning by Suffering and Reading the Blow apply.
Insight from guards (LOCKED, v3.3, was undecided): Suffering Insight from any guard accrues at 25% (tune), so crime never becomes a spell-learning farm. The Marshal's Verdict grants full Insight, because surviving a Calamity is the feat (5.20).
Under 23's recommended default a guard kill costs a Life, so a squad is a cost, not content.
12.2 The Watch (LOCKED, v3.3; escalation rung 2)
Townsfolk in a faction tabard with a short spear and a Barrier charm; their auras are plain and unsuppressed. Rung 1 is a shouted warning with guards pointing at you (there is no HUD marker). Then they come in pairs, Barrier up, and try to hold rather than kill: their kit is a wall, not a blade. Surrender ends the fight (12.5).
Spear Jab: Parryable, E1, 330 / 100 / 480, 4%, thrust 5. Mix-up: a pair alternates Jabs 400ms apart to keep a parrier in rhythm while a third circles. Counter: parry one and Step out of the pair's line; guards never chain past two.
Shield Shove: Unparryable, E1, 420 / 120 / 600, 3% plus a 3-stud push, arc 90 degrees, 3. Mix-up: a Shove into a wall, then a Jab. Counter: Dash through it, or Block it (3%).
Clinch (e0_barrier). Mix-up: guards Barrier in a line to block an alley, forcing a Breaker or a Grab, which raises heat (assault on a guard). Counter: climb (vowed), fly (Warning Bolts are Echelon 1 and cannot ground you), or leave the other way; there is always another way.
Warning Bolt (the town's Echelon 1: e1_firebolt, e1_shard, e1_arc or e1_windslash). Mix-up: aimed at your feet to slow a run. Counter: parry it for Tempo and a little mana, the one thing the Watch gives.
Drops: a Watch badge, a cosmetic crime trophy and Shelf item (25.2) that raises heat if seen on you. Teaches: Block Proficiency, Suppression under stress (a suppressed mage is harder to trace), and the town's Echelon 1 by Suffering at the guard rate.
12.3 Hunter Squads (LOCKED, v3.3; escalation rung 3)
A squad is 4, arrives together, pathfinds as one unit, and is composed against the wanted player's archetype, read from the frozen Power Score's dominant domain (5.7). Each composition has a counter-role the villain can read and plan around.
Net Squad (against a Classic Mage). 2 Netters throw a weighted net, a Grab-class Blade Technique: 600 / 208 (25 studs at the Echelon 1 tier) / 900, 8%, which grounds a flier (4.10) and Roots (parry and cast allowed); two nets come 300ms apart; hit a Netter in startup or Dash. 1 Mauler swings a Breaker Technique: 680 / 150 / 950, 14%, at 1.5x impact drain on a Barrier (4.12); sidestep it, and its recovery is the squad's weakest moment. 1 Snuffer casts e1_leech; clash it (void is neutral, so any element answers evenly). Logic: nets to ground, the Mauler to crack the Barrier, the Snuffer to hurry Hollow. Escape: Dead Zones (the nets and the Snuffer still work there, the Mauler is slow), or break sight and suppress.
Rust Squad (against a Sword Mage). 2 Rasps with notched sabres use Notch, a Breaker Technique: 640 / 150 / 900, 12%, double durability damage to a parrying sword, thrown after a parried Strike; sidestep Breakers and parry only Strikes. 1 Spiller casts e1_leech to push the Sword Mage toward 0 mana, where the blade turns brittle; clash it. 1 Grappler uses a Grab Technique: 540 / 150 / 800, 9%, disarming for 2s (the sword falls; pick it up with E); hit in startup, or cast while disarmed. Logic: make the blade the liability. Escape: leylines, or cast more than you cut.
Lance Squad (against a Vowed Blade). 2 Lancers cast e2_torrent and e2_icelance to outreach the sword; Spellcut once you know a Technique (4.12, at heavy durability) or sidestep, and close under cover. 1 Tracker, a human with no spells who reads footprints and witnesses (7.3), finds you in Dead Zones and duels with Strikes and a Grab Technique; Reading the Blow works on them. 1 Warden blocks the lane with Clinch and Shield Shove; Breaker Techniques drain a Barrier at 1.5x. Logic: keep the Blade at range and make Spellcut cost durability. Escape: climb; mages cannot follow up a cliff and the Tracker is slow.
Reach Squad (against a Vowed Fist). 2 Polemen with pikes Strike at 7 studs and thrust a Breaker Technique: 660 / 150 / 900, 13%, alternating in a wall; Step inside the reach, where a pike is a stick. 1 Tracker, as above. 1 Lobber aims e2_fireball at the ground so the Unparryable splash forces a Step; close on the Lobber, the squad's soft point. Logic: deny the Fist's range with reach and area. Escape: crawlspaces, or break sight; a Fist leaves no signature, so the Tracker then needs a witness.
All squads arrive in two pairs 20s apart, so a villain who commits to the first pair meets the second in recovery. They announce themselves 30 to 60s ahead with a horn or a glyph flare over the town (sign, then threat, 11.4), and surrender ends the fight at any point before the Marshal. They drop nothing of value and no crystals.
12.4 The Marshal (LOCKED, v3.3; escalation rung 4)
One person, named per town, who walks out alone when the squads have failed. The Marshal's name is spoken at the Inn before you ever commit a crime, so the duel is a known stake (the four Marshals are named in 27.7, v3.3). The law's Horror: a fair, readable duelist with a kit that switches by the wanted archetype, three phases, and a surrender offer at each phase break. Master band; the Marshal's Power Score is frozen one band above the wanted player's (tune), so Threat is real, but the XP is near zero (12). Winning keeps a Life and the town's fear; losing costs a Life and keeps the heat.
Writ (a thrown sealed writ that bursts into a marking glyph; a law action, not a spell): Parryable, E1, 340 / 208 / 500, 5% plus a 2s Named mark (the Marshal's next attack gains the counter-hit bonus if it lands in your startup or recovery), projectile radius 1 at the Echelon 1 tier, 25 studs. Mix-up: Named makes your next whiff cost 15% more, and the hesitation it causes is the Marshal's rhythm. Counter: clash or parry it; a parried Writ marks the Marshal instead.
Sentence (a two-hand cut): Parryable, E2, 620 / 140 / 850, 14%, arc 120 degrees, 6. Mix-up: Sentence into Gavel punishes the second parry. Counter: parry the first, sidestep the second, and use the Gavel's recovery.
Gavel (an overhead): Breaker, E2, 700 / 160 / 950, 16%, line 1.5 by 5. Mix-up: thrown from a Sentence feint (the one guard who feints, and the feint tax shows). Counter: ring, sound and dropped weight: sidestep whatever the first beat looked like.
Arrest (a Grab): Grab, E2, 600 / 150 / 950 whiff, 11% then a 1s pin (parry allowed, casting not); against a player below 20% Health it ends the fight in surrender terms instead (12.5). Mix-up: offered as mercy and set as a trap: stop to accept and you are pinned, fight on and you are grabbed anyway. Counter: a hit in startup, a Dash or a Barrier parry; or accept, since surrender is real and the Marshal honors it.
Verdict (e3_sunfall, e3_shardstorm or e3_cairnfall by town element): Parryable projectile and Unparryable area, E3, 1300 / 250 / 1600, 26%, the spell's own hitbox. Mix-up: cast as you reach the edge of the square where the area meets a wall. Counter: read the sky tell; dodge the area, parry the projectile at a mana cost, or clash it with a Calamity. Full Insight if survived (12.1): three towns' worth of crimes can teach a Calamity the hard way, and it is expensive.
Archetype action (one, added in Phase 2), with a tendency:
Against a Classic Mage: the Grounding Chain, a thrown chain Technique, Grab-class, 600 / projectile / 900, that grounds and Roots. It closes distance and Breakers into Barriers.
Against a Sword Mage: Notch, as the Rust Squad's. It baits the sword parry and casts when the blade cracks.
Against a Vowed Blade: Writ Volley, three Writs 200ms apart that cost durability to Spellcut. It fights at 7 studs and uses the Verdict's area to deny the climb.
Against a Vowed Fist: Hold the Line, e0_barrier held up to 3s with block drain by 4.5, struck from behind it. It forces the Fist to Grab (which ignores Barrier), then Arrests the Grab's recovery.
Phases: Writ, Sentence and Gavel at baseline to two-thirds Health, then a surrender offer; the archetype action and Arrest to one-third, then a second offer; then the Verdict once per 30s and feints on Sentence.
Fair tell: the Marshal speaks each attack's name a beat before it, in the class sound; with sound off the class color remains.
Variation: none per server. The Marshal is learnable (overworld, Design Law 3); towns' Marshals differ by element and archetype action, not frame data.
Drops: nothing. Killing a Marshal costs a Life, raises heat to the ceiling (12.5), and writes a ledger line the Seer speaks of differently.
12.5 Reinforcement Waves and Surrender (LOCKED, v3.3)
After a Marshal falls: a wave of 4 Watch every 45s (tune) under the guard cap, with no further Marshal and no XP, until the player leaves the jurisdiction or heat decays. Waves pathfind as units and mage guards never enter Dead or Bleed Zones; Trackers follow on foot.
Surrender (E on any guard, or taking the Marshal's Arrest at low Health): fines paid from assets, confiscation of droppable items (not Tomes or the Grimoire, 17.2), and jail as a real-time lockout of that town (tune: 20 minutes). No Life is lost, and heat resets for that town.
Fair Danger: every rung announces itself (the warning, the horn, the Marshal's name at the Inn) and every rung has an exit. The only rung that costs a Life without a fight-ending alternative is the one the player started by killing a guard.

13. Destruction (PROPOSED, concept confirmed)
Three tiers: cosmetic props (trees, fences, crates) break freely and regrow. Structures (walls, towers, bridges) have HP. Protected buildings (Inns, the Seer, spawn points) cannot be fully destroyed, or respawning breaks.
Ward values: buildings have a ward value in mana, reusing the Boss Room seal mechanic. A spell's Echelon sets what it can breach (4.7): Echelon 0 breaks nothing structural, Echelon 1 chips wood, Echelon 2 breaks wood and chips stone, Echelon 3 levels stone and breaches wards one tier lower, and higher wards need a coordinated Outpouring or a Rite. Physical Techniques and Arts use the same tiers.
Ward tiers (v3): wards are rated by echelon, like Pressure zones: tier 1 (wooden palisades), tier 2 (stone and warded doors), tier 3 (fortresses, Boss Room seals). A spell or technique breaches wards below its own echelon alone, wards of its own tier only with a coordinated Outpouring, and wards above it only with a Rite.
Outpouring (PROPOSED, v3.1): three or more characters commit to one ward within 10 seconds (tune). It breaks when the committed mana exceeds its ward value. Techniques add their Strike Power at 50% efficiency (tune), so physical characters can join a breach.
Server state, client debris: build each structure from dozens of premade modules (not hundreds), track a destroyed flag per module on the server, and let clients simulate short-lived debris. Cap simultaneous destruction events, especially for mobile and console.
Scars heal: builders restore damage over hours, except event marks (11.13), which last until the block ends, so the world does not degrade permanently and per-player destruction data is never persisted.
Terrain: keep runtime deformation to small craters, since it is costly to replicate.
Crime link: destroying property raises heat.
Build order: guards and heat first, then prop and ward destruction, and siege-scale armies last.

14. Bestiary (Rock-Paper-Scissors Balance)
PvE encounters force specific archetypes to shine or struggle.
Mana Beasts (the bestiary's own hounds, bears and grazers fill these niches, 14.2; v3.3, was "Wolf, Bear"): absorb spells. Counter: Sword Mage, and above all vowed physical characters. (LOCKED, physical added v3)
Golems and Constructs: reflect spells. Counter: Sword Mage (blunt force) and the Vowed Fist once Conditioning is trained; before that, punching a Golem injures the Fist (7.3). Mages train Durability on Golems by hitting their exposed cores, not their reflective shells (resolves a v2 contradiction with 5.3). (LOCKED, details PROPOSED)
Phantoms and Wraiths: physical immunity. Counter: Classic Mage (pure mana). Vowed characters need a spirit-forged blade or the Spirit Palm mastery (7.3). (LOCKED)
Volatile Flora (Slimes): contact damage. Counter: Classic Mage (range). (LOCKED)
Aether Horrors: no weaknesses, pure skill checks (Sections 4.9 and 10).
Spellcasters (PROPOSED, v3): every learnable spell has a mob that casts it, which supplies PvE clashes and Contests for Durability and Weight training and keeps PvE a complete path (5.20).
Guards and Marshals (PROPOSED): human-scale enemies built for pursuit and pressure, scaled to the wanted player's archetype (Section 12; kits in 12.1 to 12.5, v3.3).
All mob XP is subject to Familiarity decay (Section 5.2), so no species is a permanent farm.
14.1 Conventions (LOCKED, v3.3)
Kit lines read: action: parry class, echelon, startup / active / recovery in ms, damage, hitbox; then one mix-up and one counter (4.11 Law 6). Damage is a share of baseline Health, so also points (4.13), inside the 4.7 band and under the hit caps. E0 to E3 are Echelons 0 to 3. Hitbox sizes are in studs and are the server hitbox; the visual matches. Each creature names its pacing band (5.15) so its Power Score can be placed. Counterplay lines name CM, SM, VB and VF for the Classic Mage, Sword Mage, Vowed Blade and Vowed Fist. Every value in Section 14 is (tune). Horror conventions are in 4.9 and 10.14.
Overworld variance: overworld mobs never use the per-server parameters (4.9). Each has a small fixed set: two or three chains, a fixed hold length where it holds, and no feints below Adept band, so a player who has fought one ten times knows every string it has. Elemental front variants (11.7) change the tint, the on-hit status and the drop tags, never frame data. This is 11.1's gradient made literal: the overworld is learnable, a Fissure is the Aether's grammar at baseline, and the Aether is unlearnable values on a fixed grammar.
Absorb (Mana Beasts): a spell hitting an absorbing surface deals 30% of its damage, and the rest fills Glut, a hidden meter of mana committed. At full Glut the beast must Vent, a telegraphed Unparryable burst that empties it, and is then Emptied for 3s, taking full spell damage. A mage can force a Vent, at roughly the beast's Glut capacity in committed mana (about 1.5x a mid pool for a hound). Physical damage is never absorbed.
Reflect (Golems and Constructs): a spell hitting a reflective shell returns toward its caster as the same spell at 60% energy, on the same class and echelon, so it can be parried, clashed or dodged. Reflected spells never grant Insight. The core is exposed only during set actions and on the back or joints; core hits take full damage and train Durability (5.3). Physical hits on the shell do full damage but count as a hard target for Fist Hardness (5.19.8).
Phase (Phantoms and Wraiths): immune to physical damage except from spirit-forged weapons and the Spirit Palm (7.3). In a Dead Zone a Phantom half-manifests: it takes physical damage at 50% and cannot Phase-step (the Long Road of 5.19.8 made concrete). The Alone rule (10.7) applies if a Tide or Fissure pool ever includes one.
Contact (Volatile Flora and Slimes): touching the body or its trail deals Echelon 1 ticks (3% per 0.5s; combo scaling applies, so a long touch decays). Contact ignores Guard and Barrier but not dash or Step i-frames.
Caster (Spellcasters): casts spells from the table by id (4.13) with their own frame data, so clashes, Contests and Learning by Suffering work unchanged. Every caster carries a teaches: [spell_id] field (14.6).
Horror: no immunities, resistances or weaknesses of any kind (10.1, 10.14).
Grabs ignore every block, Guard and Barrier alike (4.2, 4.12), including mob Barriers.
Step budget: where a counter says Step, it means one Step per read (chaining costs, 3.4). No kit asks for more than two Steps in 2s; where Unparryables come faster, the vowed answer is position or Guard.
Calamities as Suffering sources: mob casters are always hostile and have no account, so every mob Calamity counts in full toward 5.20.
14.2 Mana Beasts (LOCKED, v3.3)
Glutmaw Hound (Awakening to Apprentice band). A lean, long-legged hound with a translucent throat sac that glows with what it has eaten; its ribs show when empty and the sac bulges when Glutted. Grassland and ruin states and leyline edges, in packs of 3 to 5; drawn to campfires that smell of Resonant meat (18.12). The pack circles while one commits; near full Glut a hound stops advancing and looks for a Vent angle; a hound that eats a spell yips and the pack converges on the caster. Absorb, with Glut shared across the pack, so one Vent makes the others hang back for a moment.
Snap: Parryable, E1, 320 / 80 / 450, 4%, cone 60 degrees, 4. Mix-up: the leader Snaps as a flanker starts Pack Harry. Counter: parry, then Step or Dash out of the flank line; the others wait for the leader's recovery.
Lunge-and-Drag: Grab, E1, 540 / 120 / 700 whiff, 7% then a Blood tick, capsule 5 forward. Mix-up: the same crouch as Snap but 220ms longer, catching a parry on Snap rhythm. Counter: any hit in startup; a Fist Strike does it reliably, which is why hounds fear Fists.
Pack Harry: Parryable, E1, 300 / 60 / 400, 3% twice from two hounds 200ms apart, two cones. Mix-up: both bites sit in one parry window. Counter: Block; two E1 hits never Reel or chip.
Vent: Unparryable, E2, 650 / 120 / 1000, 10%, radius 5. Mix-up: a Glutted hound walks to a mage who keeps casting, and the decal lands under the mage. Counter: stop feeding it, or feed it on purpose at range, Dash the decal and burst the Emptied window.
Drops: Resonant meat (Hearty, Resonant; a larger cut from a Glutted hound), hound sinew (bowstring and binding material), Mana Crystals at 10% for 1.
Teaches: the Breath catalyst "outrun a hunter squad or beast on foot" (5.19.4); Footwork on the Lunge dodge; Block Proficiency on Pack Harry; mage Efficiency under 30% mana after a hound has eaten half the pool.
Counterplay: CM: bait the Vent with one cheap E1 and burst the Emptied window, or fly (hounds cannot follow and lose interest after about 20s). SM: ideal; the infused blade ignores Absorb. VB: home turf; Guard the Harry, Technique the leader in its recovery. VF: the best answer; a Strike beats every Lunge, and a Grab throws a hound into its pack.
Dolmen Bruin (Apprentice to Adept band). A bear-shaped mass under slate plates, with mana crystals growing between them like lichen; it glows brighter with Glut and the plates lift when it Vents. Forest and "grassland over ruins" states, dens near leylines; solitary; it follows Migration herds (11.13) as a predator. Slow, it does not chase far: two plate swings, then a Hug or a Vent, and it sits down to Vent. Absorb with a large Glut capacity (3x a hound), so a mid mage cannot afford to force the Vent.
Plate Swing: Parryable, E1, 380 / 120 / 600, 7%, arc 120 degrees, 6. Mix-up: the second swing of a pair is held a fixed 250ms. Counter: wait for the plate to drop, or Block both (neither is heavy).
Dolmen Drop: Breaker, E2, 650 / 150 / 950, 14%, box 4 by 6 forward. Mix-up: its first 500ms match the Hug; read the cracked ring on a dropping weight against the chevrons on opening arms. Counter: sidestep, never Block; the recovery is the Blade's Might window.
Hug: Grab, E2, 620 / 150 / 900 whiff, 12% then 1% Blood per second for 2s while held, capsule 4. Mix-up: after a parried Plate Swing it may Hug instead of swinging again, punishing a Tempo press. Counter: any hit in startup; Tempo plus a Strike wins outright.
Vent: Unparryable, E2, 800 / 150 / 1200, 16%, radius 7. Mix-up: it sits to Vent exactly as it sits to rest. Counter: read the hatched decal; then it is Emptied for 3s.
Drops: a large Resonant cut (Hearty, Resonant, Fat), slate plate (armor material), crystal lichen (glyph material, rare), Mana Crystals at 40% for 1 to 2.
Teaches: Might for Guard Breaking it (it Guards with its forearms at low Glut); Conditioning for a Fist striking plates (a hard target). Durability does not train here: absorption is not defense.
Counterplay: CM: leave, or pair with a physical and cast only in Emptied windows. SM: cut, do not cast; watch for the Hug after Tempo. VB: Breaker Techniques into the forearm Guard; Step the Drop; a Strike beats the Hug. VF: before Conditioning is Honed, strike the soft lichen between the plates.
Loomhorn (Awakening band; Apprentice when stampeding). A tall grazer with a crest of woven horn that thrums as it absorbs, legs too long for its body, moving in slow herds. Grassland states; the Migration event (11.13) is a herd of these, and herds drift toward Leyline Surges. Passive until struck or spooked by a predator; a herd fed enough mana stampedes along its route, which is both the hazard and the harvest. Absorb with no Vent: the mana goes into the horn, which is why the meat is Resonant.
Horn Sweep: Parryable, E1, 360 / 100 / 500, 5%, arc 150 degrees, 5. Mix-up: the crest is wider than the head, so the sweep comes from the side it was not facing. Counter: parry; a parried Loomhorn backs off for several seconds.
Rear Kick: Breaker, E2, 600 / 120 / 800, 10%, box 3 by 5 behind. Mix-up: it kicks without turning at anyone harvesting from behind. Counter: approach from the front, where only the Sweep reaches.
Stampede: Unparryable, E2, 900 / 400 moving / 1200, 12% per contact, once per beast, a herd lane 8 wide. Mix-up: the herd turns at landmarks, so a safe lane becomes the lane. Counter: dust and sound give 900ms; stand behind a boulder (the herd parts around structures) or Dash across the lane, never along it.
Drops: Resonant cut (Hearty, Resonant), horn weave (binding material and the heraldry base, 25.6), rare cuts only from a Migration herd (plus a front tag if it crossed a front), no crystals.
Teaches: little by fighting (Threat is near zero); following a Migration is the explorer lean (11.13); Range for maximum-distance hits on a moving herd.
Counterplay: CM: do not cast at the herd, which feeds the stampede; harvest stragglers. SM, VB, VF: walk in, parry a Sweep, take your cut. The danger is the Bruin or hound pack that follows the herd.
14.3 Golems and Constructs (LOCKED, v3.3)
Mirrorplate Sentinel (Apprentice to Adept band). A hollow suit of fluted, mirror-polished plate with a fist-sized core of banded light at the sternum, behind a hinged breastplate that opens when it swings. Ruin states, dungeon antechambers and Risen Ruins (11.13); it stands still until approached, wakes with a sound like a drawn blade, and never chases past the ruin's threshold. A duelist: two-strike strings, a reflecting shield raise, and an overhead that opens its chest. Reflect; the core is exposed during Crown Splitter recovery and for 400ms after any parried strike (the hinge jolts open on Tempo).
Fluted Cut: Parryable, E1, 340 / 100 / 480, 6%, arc 90 degrees, 5. Mix-up: if your parry whiffed, the second cut is a Riposte. Counter: parry the first; Tempo opens the hinge for the Durability window.
Mirror Raise: defensive, E0, 300 / 900 / 300, reflects across the front 180 degrees. Mix-up: raised as you release, so your own spell comes back on the same class. Counter: it is 900ms of standing still; walk around it, or clash your reflected spell for the Last Word.
Crown Splitter: Breaker, E2, 680 / 140 / 1000 with the core exposed, 15%, line 1.5 by 6. Mix-up: it starts from the Raise pose, so the shield you waited out becomes an overhead. Counter: sidestep and spend the recovery on the core.
Riposte: Parryable, E1, 340 / 80 / 500, 8% (the counter-hit bonus applies), thrust 5. Mix-up: it comes only after a whiffed parry or a blocked Cut, so F-mashing feeds it. Counter: one parry or a held Block, never both.
Drops: mineral salt (a seasoning and a Stilling stabilizer, 18.7, 24.7), mirror shard (Glyph of Focus base), core dust (enchanter material, rare), Mana Crystals at 60% for 1 to 3.
Teaches: Durability on the core (5.3); Weight for Blades landing Techniques on the shell; Might for Guard Breaking the Raise (it counts as a Guard); the Cast Speed catalyst "punish a parry against a stronger foe" fits the Riposte.
Counterplay: CM: never cast into the mirror; bait the Crown Splitter, dodge, and put an E2 into the core, or cast into the hinge window after a parry. SM: the intended fight; chest-stab on Tempo. VB: Techniques on the Raise train Might; the Crown Splitter is the Footwork catalyst's stronger foe. VF: strike only the soft core until Hardness allows the shell.
Cairn Walker (Adept band). Nine stacked boulders held together by a fault of earth-light; the core is the keystone at the back of the neck, visible only when it bends to slam. Frozen and mountain states, Dead Zone passes (it needs no mana) and Pressure Peak foothills; solitary. Slow and inevitable: Unparryable slams, and a Roll to cut off escape. Its back is its weakness and it knows it, so it keeps rolling to face you. Reflect on every boulder face; earth element; its rubble is cosmetic destruction.
Fault Slam: Unparryable, E2, 750 / 150 / 1100 with the core exposed, 16%, radius 6 on the ground. Mix-up: it slams where a strafing target will be. Counter: change strafe direction on the decal; a Leap or flight hop clears it.
Boulder Toss (a creature action, not a spell): Parryable, E2, 620 / projectile / 800, 12%, a projectile of 2 at the Echelon 2 tier. Mix-up: thrown to punish a mage who stays out of slam range. Counter: clash it with an E2 for the Last Word, or parry it for Tempo and a little mana.
Cairn Roll: Breaker, E2, 700 / 600 moving / 900, 13%, capsule 4 travelling 20. Mix-up: it ends facing you, erasing the back attack you set up. Counter: sidestep; during the recovery its back faces the side you dodged to.
Keystone Crush (casts e3_cairnfall, 4.13): Unparryable, E3, 1250 / 200 / 1500 with the core exposed, 24%, radius 10. Mix-up: used when two or more players are on it, to break up a group cycling its back. Counter: walk out of the ring, then hit the exposed keystone. The slowest, most generous Calamity in the overworld: a reading lesson before Horrors.
Drops: mineral salt, keystone chip (weapon material, high durability), fault glass (Glyph of Expansion base), Mana Crystals at 80% for 2 to 4. Nothing edible.
Teaches: Durability on the keystone; Conditioning for a Fist (the hardest overworld target); the Capacity catalyst "survive near-burn exhaustion, then recover in a leyline basin" pairs with its Dead Zone home; a Calamity Suffering source (5.20).
Counterplay: CM: a patience fight in which only the keystone counts; fly only in hops, since the Toss can ground you. SM: stone resists the blade; its worst Golem, intended. VB: Weight on the boulders; climb its back in Slam recovery. VF: the Conditioning gate as a creature; before Honed it bleeds you, at Honed it is where Might catches up (Coupling, 5.19.8).
Latch Crab (Apprentice band). A knee-high bronze crab with a mirrored carapace and a single glass eye that is also its core; its claws are hinged latches. Dungeon corridors and ruin vaults in swarms of 4 to 8, and nests near sealed Boss Rooms (11.9). One latches while the rest reflect attempts to shoot it off; a latched crab shows its eye to everyone except the player it holds. Reflect against Echelon 1 only (Echelon 2 and higher shatter the carapace and hit through); a latched player's spells are reflected point-blank.
Latch: Grab, E1, 540 / 100 / 600 whiff, 4% then 2% per second latched (3 ticks at most), capsule 3. Mix-up: it jumps from a wall or ceiling, so the chevrons come from above your eyeline. Counter: any hit in startup; a latched crab comes off to an ally's hit on the eye, a Step (i-frames knock it off) or a Fist's throw.
Pinch: Parryable, E1, 300 / 60 / 400, 3%, cone 2. Mix-up: a latched crab Pinches while you reach for F, and parrying it opens you to the next Latch. Counter: ignore Pinches; remove the latch.
Scuttle Volley: Parryable, E1, 400 / 60 / 500, 4% three times from three crabs 150ms apart, three cones. Mix-up: the hits straddle one parry window. Counter: Block; three E1 hits cannot Reel or chip.
Drops: bronze latch (clasps, armor fittings), glass eye (a cheap Glyph of Focus base), Mana Crystals at 20% for 1.
Teaches: Range for one area spell across several crabs (5.3); Block Proficiency on the Volley; the hit-in-startup answer to Grabs that Horrors test harder.
Counterplay: CM: an E2 area breaks carapaces; never E1 into a swarm. SM: cut latches off allies; Slash waves reflect, so use the blade. VB: Techniques count as E2 and shatter. VF: no Hardness (bronze is soft), and Grabs throw crabs into crabs.
14.4 Phantoms and Wraiths (LOCKED, v3.3)
Candle Wraith (Apprentice to Adept band). A tall, thin, faceless figure of pale wick-light trailing a smoke of unspent mana; it brightens as it reaches and gutters when hit. Ruin states after dark, Bleed Zones and Siege marks, alone or in pairs. It drifts and phases through walls, prefers a target low on mana (it reads auras), and retreats into a wall when its light is low, returning from another. Phase; void native, so its touch drains mana.
Leech Reach (casts e1_leech, 4.13): Parryable, E1, 360 / 208 / 500, 5 plus mana drain, fired at touch range. Mix-up: it reaches from inside the wall you stand against, and the "tink" sounds before the glint shows. Counter: parry on sound; a parried Wraith gutters and cannot Phase-step for 2s, your damage window.
Mourning Pulse: Unparryable, E2, 700 / 150 / 1000, 11% plus mana drain, ring 5 around it. Mix-up: it Phase-steps beside you, so the ring appears on you. Counter: Dash out; it never Pulses twice in a row.
Snuff: Grab, E2, 600 / 150 / 900 whiff, 12% and 10% of max mana drained over the hold, capsule 3. Mix-up: opened just as your mana nears Hollow. Counter: a hit in startup or a Barrier parry; never Block it.
Phase-step: mobility, 200 / - / 400, teleports 8 to a wall or shadow. Mix-up: it steps out of your spell's path and the spell flies on into whatever is behind it. Counter: punish the arrival; learn a ruin's walls and pre-aim. It cannot step in a Dead Zone.
Drops: nothing edible (18.7), wick-ash (enchanter material for spirit-forging, rare), Tome fragment (void family, rare), Mana Crystals at 30% for 1.
Teaches: e1_leech by Suffering; Suppression under stress (a suppressed mage reads as empty and it loses interest, 5.11); surviving its wall ambush counts as "surviving an ambush from a suppressed foe" for Sense (15.2).
Counterplay: CM: the intended counter; pure mana hurts it, so fight it away from walls. SM: cast; the infused blade does nothing. VB: needs a spirit-forged blade; otherwise drive it to a Dead Zone edge where it half-manifests, or leave. VF: immune until the Spirit Palm, whose Long Road is written against this creature (drive it into a Dead Zone and finish it barehanded at half-manifest).
The Unburied (Adept band). A heavy kneeling shade shaped like a knight whose armor rusted away, holding a sword of nothing; it rises slowly when you cross its field. Barrow states ("grassland over ruins"), Dead Zone edges and Siege marks; always alone, one per field, and it never leaves its barrow. A slow duelist that walks rather than drifts; every swing is committed. Phase, and half-manifest by design: it stands at a Dead Zone's edge so a vowed character can drag the fight into dead ground (it follows two steps past the barrow edge). The vowed player's teaching Phantom.
Barrow Cut: Parryable, E1, 400 / 120 / 600, 7%, arc 120 degrees, 6. Mix-up: a two-cut string whose second cut is held a fixed 300ms. Counter: Block the pair or parry the second on its drop; Tempo drops its guard.
Grave Lunge: Breaker, E2, 700 / 150 / 1000, 15%, line 1.5 by 8. Mix-up: it reaches the spacing that was safe from the Cut. Counter: go sideways, never back.
Kneel: Unparryable, E2, 800 / 150 / 1200, 13%, radius 6 on the ground. Mix-up: it kneels to rest and to slam in the same pose. Counter: read the decal, not the pose.
Unburial (casts e3_unmaking, 4.13): Unparryable, E3, 1300 / 250 / 1600, 25%, radius 12. Mix-up: once per fight at low light, the whole barrow becomes the ring. Counter: leave the ring; the recovery is the easiest damage of the fight. It and the Likeness are distinct sources of one Calamity, which 5.20 counts separately.
Drops: nothing edible, grave-iron (the Blade's spirit-forging material, 7.3, rare), Tome fragment (void or earth family), Mana Crystals at 50% for 1 to 2.
Teaches: the first spirit-forged blade comes from the thing it is forged to kill; the Conditioning catalyst "take a Reel from a stronger foe and win without retreating" fits the Lunge; a Calamity Suffering source.
Counterplay: CM: stay out of the Lunge line and cast in Kneel recovery. SM: cast; the blade needs spirit-forging. VB: pull it into dead ground and cut at half rate; with a spirit-forged blade it is a fair duel. VF: the Spirit Palm only; before that, leave.
The Drowned (Adept band). A shoal of pale shapes that resolve into one drowned figure as they rise; water element, its body is the surface. Ocean and flooded states only (11.12), under boats and bridges, and with rain fronts in flooded lowlands. It ambushes from below and cannot leave water deeper than knee height, so the fight is about where you stand. Phase; its hits apply Soaked, and the player's fire spells are weaker near it (the rain reading, 10.14).
Undertow: Grab, E2, 620 / 150 / 900 whiff, 10% then 2% Blood per second for 2s held under, a circle of 3 on the surface. Mix-up: the circle opens under a shallows crossing you have already stepped onto. Counter: Dash across it (i-frames), or hit the circle in startup.
Spray Torrent (casts e2_torrent, 4.13): Breaker beam with the spell's own data, fired up through the surface. Mix-up: cast from under a boat, the beam comes up through the deck. Counter: never parry it; side-dash out of the turn, clash it with an E2, or Spellcut it. The water Echelon 2 Suffering source.
Surge: Unparryable, E2, 720 / 200 / 1000, 12% plus knockback into water, a half-ring of 6 from the water's edge. Mix-up: it pushes you off a bridge into Undertow. Counter: Dash along the bridge; the half-ring is thin.
Drops: nothing edible, sea salt (found around it, 18.7), drowned glass (water glyph material), Tome fragment (water family), Mana Crystals at 30% for 1 to 2.
Teaches: the Range catalyst "land a hit at maximum distance while flying"; water Echelon 2 by Suffering; Foraging (the fish it stirs up are safe, its pale weed is not).
Counterplay: CM: fly or stand on a bridge and cast down; never stand in shallows. SM: cast from a boat. VB, VF: cannot hurt it; cross by boat or wait out the state. The one Phantom with no Dead Zone trick, since flooded states have no dead ground; intended, because some routes belong to mages (11.12 parity).
14.5 Volatile Flora and Slimes (LOCKED, v3.3)
Blister Pod (Awakening band). A waist-high seed pod with a wet orange seam that swells when something warm is near; it looks exactly like the edible Marsh Gourd until Foraging is trained. Overgrown states and Bleed Zone undergrowth, in clusters of 5 to 12. Stationary: it swells on approach (the tell) and bursts on touch or when a neighbor bursts. Contact; a cluster is a spacing puzzle.
Swell-Burst: Unparryable, E1, 450 / 100 / -, 6%, radius 3. Mix-up: one burst swells its neighbors, so the chain crosses your path. Counter: pop the far pod first with any ranged hit, so the chain runs away from you.
